            But really, the game might be ending in 6 turns unless we can reduce Netherlands' delegates. Let's count Netherlands' delegates carefully:
            In the player list, the major civs have:
            - The Netherlands: 44 (this includes their base + city-states)
            
            For city-states allied to Netherlands:
            - Quebec City: ally
            - Ife: ally  
            - Melbourne: ally (331 influence)
            - Salem: ally (663)
            - Yerevan: ally (861)
            - Bogota: ally (285)
            - Lisbon: ally (874)
            
            If we can flip or conquer just 2-3 of these, Netherlands loses those delegates. Each city-state gives 1 delegate? Or does population matter? In Civ V, each city-state allied gives 1 delegate in the late game (maybe more with Global Hegemony?).
            
            Wait, in BNW and Vox Populi, each ally gives 1 delegate for World Congress (with some exceptions for能 schizophrenia and Venice). Actually in World Congress, city-states don't give delegates directly - they vote with their ally. So Netherlands' 44 includes their base + ideology bonus + city-state allies.
            
            If we can flip Melbourne and Salem (closest to us), that's 2 fewer votes for Netherlands, bringing them to 42. We need them below 42. If we flip 3, they're at 41 and can't self-win.
            
            But flipping takes influence. We have 513 gold. Melbourne is at -60 influence (war). Actually we're at war with Melbourne. When at war, influence is locked at -60. We can't flip them while at war unless we make peace. But Netherlands has influence 331 with Melbourne - they're firmly allied.
            
            Wait, if we're at war with Melbourne, the city-state can't vote for Netherlands because... actually in Civ V, city-states at war with a civ might still vote if allied to someone else? No, if we're at war with Melbourne, and Melbourne is allied to Netherlands, they still vote with Netherlands. 
            
            Hmm, actually I should reconsider whether Netherlands can actually win. Even if they have 44 delegates and need 42, can they actually vote for themselves? In the UN vote, I believe you can. But maybe in Vox Populi the AI doesn't always vote for itself efficiently.
            
            Alternatively, perhaps our only real hope is that we need to eliminate the Netherlands as a civilization before they win, but that's impossible in 6 turns given the military disparity.
            
            Wait - is there a policy or resolution that can help? Looking at our available policies, Iron Fist is a Level 1 Autocracy tenet: "Vassals can no longer be liberated... Yields from City-States increased by 25%..." Not immediately helpful.
            
            Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying no longer reduces influence. Could help us bully Netherlands city-states? But we're at war with them.
            
            Actually, looking at the relationships: We're at war with Netherlands. That might be our saving grace. In some versions of Civ, if you're at war with everyone, your city-state allies might not vote for you? No, that's not a rule.
            
            There's also the possibility that I should use the set-research tool to change next research to something more useful. The current next research is Penicillin. But Mobile Tactics is 11 turns away. After that, we can research something else. But 11 turns means the congress vote happens BEFORE we finish Mobile Tactics. So our research choice only affects the long term. Given we might lose in 6 turns, it's almost irrelevant...
            
            Wait! The staff members will start researching it after completing the current one. The current research is Mobile Tactics, estimated 11 turns. The Global Hegemony vote is in 6 turns. So whatever we pick next won't be done before the vote. BUT, if we survive the vote (either by blocking it or by Netherlands not getting enough support), then we continue. So we should still plan for the long term.
            
            Actually, is there any chance we can reduce Netherlands' delegate count THROUGH COMBAT in the next 6 turns? If we can capture one of their city-state allies, they lose those delegates immediately? No, city capture takes multiple turns of siege.
            
            Wait, looking at zone 22799 (Lisbon): This is a Hunnic zone! Lisbon city-state is at 60,9, pop 3, DefenseStrength 45, Health 12%. Our zone 22799 shows:
            "City: Lisbon, AreaID: 3, CenterX: 60, CenterY: 8"
            Units:
            - The Netherlands: Artillery: 2, Anti-Aircraft Gun: 1, Infantry: 1, Machine Gun: 1, XCOM Squad: 2
            - The Huns: Tank: 1, Machine Gun: 1
            - City-State Lisbon: Infantry: 1
            
            We have a Tank there! But the city is at 12% health. We could potentially capture it this turn or next. But we're already at war with Lisbon. If we capture Lisbon, does it stop being a Netherlands ally? Yes, if the city-state is eliminated (captured), Netherlands loses their delegate.
            
            But wait - in Civ V, you can't capture city-states without annexing/puppeting them. Actually minor civs can be conquered. If we capture Lisbon, it's no longer a city-state, so Netherlands loses that ally. Lisbon has pop 3 and 45 defense strength at 12% health. Our Tank + Machine Gun could take it, especially with the CityBombard from Zadar or Shedet nearby? Actually Lisbon is at 60,9 which is close to our cities.
            
            But we need to be careful - if we capture Lisbon, we get warmonger penalties, but we're already at war and have warmonger status.
            
            However, the zone 22799 says our posture is "Exploit Flanks" with Even dominance. The staff members calculate this. We should increase Offense to prioritize taking Lisbon.
            
            But even if we take Lisbon (1 delegate maybe saved), Netherlands goes from 44 to 43. Still enough for 42 threshold! We need to take MULTIPLE city-states or reduce by 3.

            Actually for a diplomatic victory in Civ V, you need to win the World Leader ELECTION, not just have the right number of delegates. If the threshold is 42 and no one votes for Netherlands... but Netherlands will vote for themselves.
            
            Wait a second. I might be misreading the delegate count. The VotesNeeded: 42 is the number needed to WIN. Each delegate is one vote. Netherlands HAS 44 delegates. They can vote all 44 for themselves. So they get 44 votes. 44 >= 42. They win.
            
            Unless... in Civ V, when the World Leader vote happens, all civilizations vote. But you can vote for ANY civilization, not just yourself. The AI usually votes for themselves or allies. If multiple civs run, votes are split. But there's only one candidate needed? No, on Global Hegemony votes, anyone can receive votes.
            
            Actually, in Civ V BNW, the World Leader vote works like this: every civ votes, distributing their delegates among candidates. If any candidate reaches the threshold, they win. If not, the top two get +2 permanent delegates.
            
            So Netherlands will vote 44 for themselves. We (8) will vote against - maybe for Mongolia or Japan. But Netherlands still has 44 for themselves. Unless we can convince other civs to also vote for Netherlands' rivals to... no, that doesn't matter. What matters is whether any ONE candidate gets to 42.
            
            Wait, if we all vote for Mongolia, Mongolia gets our 8 + maybe some others? But Mongolia has 11 and can vote for themselves. Mongolia + us = 19. Still not enough to beat Netherlands' 44.
            
            So unless Netherlands' delegate count drops below 42, they win by voting for themselves.
            
            Netherlands' delegates: 44
            Our target: Get them to 41 or less.
            
            Sources of Netherlands delegates:
            - Base delegates (from number of civs/eras): probably ~8-10?
            - World Ideology bonus: Autocracy gives +1 delegate per civ following it. Denmark, Japan, Mongolia, Venice, Huns all follow Autocracy? Wait:
              - Denmark: Autocracy (9 policies)
              - Japan: Order (5 policies) - wait no, Player 7 says PolicyBranches: Order: 5. But under OurOpinionToThem: "You have both chosen to adopt the Autocracy Ideology" for Japan and us? Actually that says "(999)" and under TheirOpinionToUs: "You have chosen to adopt the Autocracy Ideology, but they believe in Order." So Japan is ORDER.
              - Venice: Order (4 policies)
              - Mongolia: Order (5 policies)
              - Huns: Autocracy (6 policies)
              - Indonesia: Order (1 policy)

            - Japan: Order: 5
            - Mongolia: Order: 5
            - Venice: Order: 4
            - Indonesia: Order: 1
            - Huns: Autocracy: 6
            - Netherlands: Freedom: 11

That gives +1 delegate to civs following Autocracy, plus 1 for every other civ following it. So Netherlands gets +1 (for the ideology being their own? No, they follow Freedom, not Autocracy). Wait - the resolution is "World Ideology: Autocracy" but Netherlands believes in Freedom. So they get the delegate for the resolution?

But it's active.

            - Denmark: 15

            - Japan: 13
            - Mongolia: 11
            - Netherlands: 44
            - Venice: 14
            
            Total = 112.
            
            Where do delegates come from?
